{I_{\star}}=[m]
.
Under this assumption, the matrix
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}
in Equation (
24
) ican be written as

{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}=\beta^{2}\left[\boldsymbol{J}-\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{J}_{1}&\\
&\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right].

The expectation of
\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}
is given by

\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}=\beta^{2}\left[\boldsymbol{J}-\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{J}_{1}&\\
&\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right]+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=\left(\sigma_{0}^{2}+\beta^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{J}-\beta^{2}\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{J}_{1}&\\
&\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\end{pmatrix}+\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right),

where
\boldsymbol{J}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
and
\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
are matrices of all ones.
By Equation (
118
),

\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\right\rangle.

Expanding
\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}
and
using the SDP constraint
\langle\boldsymbol{J},\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{J},\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\rangle=K^{2}
, it follows that

\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\right\rangle\leq-\beta^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{J}_{1}&\\
&\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\rangle.

(119)

By the definition of
\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}
in Equation (
23
), since
\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}
is all one over its support
I_{\star}^{c}\times I_{\star}^{c}
, we have

\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{J}_{1}&\\
&\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\end{pmatrix}-\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle=0,\quad\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle=K^{2}.

(120)

Additionally, by the constraint
\langle\boldsymbol{J},\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\rangle=K^{2}
in Equation (
22
), we have

\langle\boldsymbol{J},\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\rangle=0.

(121)

it follows from Equations (
119
)-(
121
) that

\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\right\rangle\leq\beta^{2}\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{J}_{1}&\\
&\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\end{pmatrix}-\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\rangle.

Rearranging terms, it follows that

\displaystyle 2\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=m+1}^{n}\widehat{Z}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{J}-\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{J}_{1}&\\
&\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle

(122)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\rangle.

Applying Lemma
20
to the left hand side of Equation (
122
)

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}\leq\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle,

which yields Equation (
27
).

Expanding
\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}
as

\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}=\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}+2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J},

it follows that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}\left|\left\langle\!\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\!\!-\!\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right|+\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}\left|\left\langle\!\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\!\!-\!\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle\right|.

Applying Hölder’s inequality, and using the fact that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
trivially,

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}

(123)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right|+\frac{10}{\beta^{2}}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right|+\frac{10}{\beta^{2}}\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\right\|_{1}.

By construction of
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}
, we have

\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\|_{1}\leq\beta\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{1}\leq\beta n\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\sigma\beta r^{1/2}n^{3/2},

where the last inequality follows from the conditioning on
\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{rn}\}
.
Additionally, we have

\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{1}=\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}nr

and, by Assumption
2
,

\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\|_{1}\lesssim\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}
.
Applying the triangle inequality, it follows that

\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\right\|_{1}\leq\beta n\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\sigma\beta r^{1/2}n^{3/2},+\sigma^{2}nr+\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}.

Applying this bound to Equation (
123
),

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right|+\frac{\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}+\sigma^{2}nr}{\beta^{2}}+\frac{\sigma r^{1/2}n^{3/2}}{\beta},

as we set out to show.
∎

D.2
Proof of Lemma
3

Proof.

By the triangle inequality,

\left|\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\rangle\right|\leq\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2},

(124)

where

\alpha_{1}=\left|\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\rangle\right|\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha_{2}=\left|\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\rangle\right|.

We will thus bound these two quantities in turn.
Both bounds are based on Bernstein’s inequality and Grothendieck’s inequality
(
Vershynin, 2018
)
.

Applying Grothendieck’s inequality as quoted in Equation (
28
),

\alpha_{1}\leq 2c_{0}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i,j}\left(C^{(1)}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}C^{(1)}_{ij}\right)x_{i}y_{j}\right|.

Expanding
\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}
using Equation (
26
)
and bounding
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
,

\alpha_{1}\leq 4c_{0}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i,j}\left((\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})^{2}-\sigma^{2}_{ij}\right)x_{i}y_{j}\right|+4c_{0}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i,j}B_{ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}x_{i}y_{j}\right|.

(125)

We will bound the two right-hand terms in Equation (
125
) separately.

For
i,j\in[n]
, define
X_{ij}=[(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})^{2}-\sigma_{ij}^{2}]x_{i}y_{j}
.
By Assumption
1
on the magnitude of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}
, we have

|X_{ij}|=\left|(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})^{2}-\sigma_{ij}^{2}\right|\lesssim L^{2},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}X_{ij}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})^{4}\leq L^{2}\sigma^{2}.

Applying Bernstein’s inequality stated in Lemma
38
, for any
t<n^{2}\sigma^{2}
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n}X_{ij}\right|\geq t\right]\leq 2\exp\left(-c\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{n^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{2}},\frac{t}{L^{2}}\right\}\right)=2\exp\left\{-\frac{ct^{2}}{n^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{2}}\right\}.

Applying a union bound over all possible choices of
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i,j}\left((\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})^{2}-\sigma^{2}_{ij}\right)x_{i}y_{j}\right|\geq t\right]

\displaystyle\leq~\sum_{\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{i,j}\left((\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})^{2}-\sigma_{ij}^{2}\right)x_{i}y_{j}\right|\geq t\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim~4^{n}\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{n^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{n^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{2}}+n\right).

Noting that
n^{3/2}\sigma L\ll n^{2}\sigma^{2}
by Equation (
4
) and taking
t=Cn^{3/2}\sigma L
for some sufficiently large constant
C>0
, we obtain that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-40})
,

\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i,j}\left((\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})^{2}-\sigma_{ij}^{2}\right)x_{i}y_{j}\right|\lesssim\sigma Ln^{3/2}.

(126)

Using the same arguement as above, for any
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
, under Assumption
1
we have

\left|B^{\star}_{ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}x_{i}y_{j}\right|\leq\beta L,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\left(B^{\star}_{ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}x_{i}y_{j}\right)^{2}\leq\beta^{2}\sigma^{2}.

Applying Bernstein’s inequality as stated in Lemma
38
followed by a union bound over
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}
, it holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-40})
that

\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq n}B^{\star}_{ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}x_{i}y_{j}\right|\lesssim\max\left\{m^{1/2}n\beta\sigma,\beta Ln\right\}\lesssim n^{3/2}\beta\sigma,

(127)

where the last inequality follows from Equation (
4
) and the fact that
m\leq n
.

Applying Equations (
126
) and (
127
) to Equation (
125
) and using the assumption that
\beta\leq L
,

\alpha_{1}\lesssim\sigma n^{3/2}L.

(128)

Again applying Grothendieck’s inequality,

\alpha_{2}\lesssim\max_{\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm\}^{n}}\left|\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}x_{i}y_{j}\right|.

(129)

Noting that

\left|\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}x_{i}y_{j}\right|\leq\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty}L\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}x_{i}y_{j}\right)^{2}\leq\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}^{2}\sigma^{2},

applying Bernstein’s inequality as stated in Lemma
38
yields that for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}x_{i}y_{j}\right|\geq t\right]\leq 2\exp\left(-c\min\left\{\frac{t}{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty}L},\frac{t^{2}}{\sigma^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\right\}\right).

Applying the union bound and choosing a suitable
t
, it follows that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-40})

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}}\left|\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}x_{i}y_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\left\{Ln\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty},\sigma\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\sqrt{n}\right\}\lesssim\max\left\{\sigma Ln,\sigma^{2}nr^{1/2}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}

where the second inequality holds as we conditioned on the event
\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{rn}\}\cap\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty}\leq C\sigma\}
, the last inequality follows from Equation (
4
) and the fact that
r\leq n
.
Applying this bound to Equation (
129
) yields

\alpha_{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}.

(130)

Applying Equations (
128
) and (
130
) to Equation (
124
)

\left|\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\rangle\right|\lesssim\sigma n^{3/2}L+\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}\leq\sigma n^{3/2}L,

(131)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-40})
conditioned on the event
\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{rn}\}\cap\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty}\leq C\sigma\}
, as we set out to show, where the last inequality follows from the assumption in Equation (
4
).
∎

D.3
Lemma
14
and Proof

Lemma 14
.

Suppose that
\beta\leq L
,
n>2m
and that the conditions in Lemma
2
hold.
Conditional on the event

\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|\leq C\sigma\sqrt{n}\right\}\cap\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{C\sigma\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\},

(132)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-7})
, we have

|S_{2}|\lesssim\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}}{n}+\frac{\sigma L}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1},

where
S_{2}
is defined in Equation (
32
).

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}
is defined in Equation (
33
).
Expanding the definition of
S_{2}
,

\displaystyle|S_{2}|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}),\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}),\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma^{2}_{0}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle\right|

(133)

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}})\right)\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\right\|+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right\|\right),

where the last inequality holds by the matrix Hölder inequality and the fact that
\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}})\succeq 0
.
By the triangle inequality,

\left\|\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\right\|\lesssim\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|+\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|+\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|+\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\|+\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\|.

(134)

By basic properties of matrix norms,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\left(\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}^{4}\right)^{1/2}\leq\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(135)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}.

By Assumption
1
,

\left|(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})^{2}-\sigma_{ij}^{2}\right|\leq 2L^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\left((\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})^{2}-\sigma_{ij}^{2}\right)^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}L^{2}.

The matrix version of Bernstein’s inequality, as stated in Theorem
25
, implies that for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|\geq 4\sigma L\sqrt{n}+t\right]\leq n\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{L^{4}}\right).

Taking
t=CL^{2}\log^{1/2}n
for suitably large constant
C
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-8})
,

\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|\lesssim\sigma L\sqrt{n}+L^{2}\log^{1/2}n\lesssim\sigma L\sqrt{n}.

(136)

By basic properties of the norm and Equation (
132
),

\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|\leq\beta\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|\leq\beta\sigma\sqrt{n}.

(137)

By Lemma
36
, we have

\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\right\|\lesssim\sigma\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}+L\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\log n}=\sigma\beta\sqrt{n}+L\beta\sqrt{\log n}

(138)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{2,\infty}+L\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\log n}

(139)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}r^{1/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}+\frac{\sigma L\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r\log^{1/2}n}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}.

Applying Equations (
135
) through (
139
) to Equation (
134
), under the assumption that
\beta\leq L
and the growth rate assumption on
\sigma,L
in Equation (
4
)

\|\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}+\sigma L\sqrt{n}.

Additionally, under Assumption
2
, we have

\left\|\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\right\|+\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}+\sigma L\sqrt{n}.

Applying this bound to Equation (
133
),

|S_{2}|\leq\left[\sigma^{2}\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}+\sigma L\sqrt{n}\right]\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}})\right).

Applying Lemma
21
,

|S_{2}|\leq\left[\sigma^{2}\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}+\sigma L\sqrt{n}\right]\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}}{K}.

By
K=n-m
and
m<n/3
, the above bound implies the result stated in Lemma
14
.
∎

D.4
Lemma
15
and Proof

Lemma 15
.

Under the conditions in Lemmas
2
and
3
hold, suppose that the event

\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{nr},\;\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{C\sigma\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}},\;\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{2,\infty}\leq C\sigma r^{1/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}\right\},

(140)

holds for some sufficiently large constant
C>0
.
Then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-7})
, the quantity
S_{1}
defined in Equation (
31
) satisfies

\displaystyle|S_{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}\left(\beta\sqrt{\frac{r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)}{n}}+\sigma\frac{r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)}{n}+L\sqrt{\frac{\log\left(n^{2}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}^{-1}\right)}{n}}\right)

Proof.

Recalling the definition of
S_{1}
and applying the triangle inequality, we have

|S_{1}|\leq\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\right)\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right)\right\rangle\right|.

(141)

We remind the reader that
\mathcal{P}_{T}
is defined in Equation (
33
).

Consider two upper-triangular matrices:

\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}=[\xi^{(1)}_{ij}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
with entries

\xi^{(1)}_{ij}=\begin{cases}2(\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B^{\star}_{ij})\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}&\text{ if }1\leq i<j\leq n\\
0&\text{ otherwise }\\
\end{cases}

(142)

and
\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)}=[\xi^{(2)}_{ij}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
with entries

\xi^{(2)}_{ij}=\begin{cases}(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})^{2}-\sigma^{2}_{ij}&\text{ if }1\leq i<j\leq n\\
\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ii})^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}_{ii}&\text{ if }1\leq i=j\leq n\\
0&\text{ if }1\leq j<i\leq n.\end{cases}

(143)

Expanding out the Hadamard products, one may verify that

2({\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}+\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right)=\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)\top}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)\top},

and we can decompose
\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}
as

\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}=\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)\top}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)\top}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+2{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}.

(144)

Defining the quantities

\displaystyle S_{11}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)\top})\right\rangle

(145)

\displaystyle S_{12}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)\top})\right\rangle~~~\text{ and }

\displaystyle S_{13}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+2{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\right\rangle,

applying the decomposition in Equation (
144
) and applying the triangle inequality,

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\right)\right\rangle\right|\leq|S_{11}|+|S_{12}|+|S_{13}|.

(146)

We will proceed to bound these three terms separately.
Throughout the proof, we will frequently use the equalities

\left\langle\boldsymbol{A}_{1},\boldsymbol{A}_{2}\boldsymbol{A}_{3}\right\rangle=\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{A}_{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}_{2}\boldsymbol{A}_{3})=\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{A}_{3}\boldsymbol{A}_{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}_{2})=\left\langle\boldsymbol{A}_{1}\boldsymbol{A}_{3}^{\top},\boldsymbol{A}_{2}\right\rangle

and

\left\langle\boldsymbol{A}_{1},\boldsymbol{A}_{2}\right\rangle=\operatorname{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{A}_{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}_{2}\right)=\operatorname{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{A}_{2}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}_{1}\right)=\left\langle\boldsymbol{A}_{2},\boldsymbol{A}_{1}\right\rangle

which holds for any compatible matrices
\boldsymbol{A}_{1},\boldsymbol{A}_{2}
and
\boldsymbol{A}_{3}
.

Recalling the definition of
S_{11}
from Equation (
145
), the definition of
\mathcal{P}_{T}
in Equation (
33
) and
K=n-m
, we have

\displaystyle S_{11}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)\top})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=2\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}}{K}+\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)\top}}{K}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K^{2}}+\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K^{2}}\right\rangle.

Since

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{1}{K^{2}}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\rangle=\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K},\frac{1}{K}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}\right\rangle

and

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{1}{K^{2}}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}\right\rangle,

we have

S_{11}=2\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}+\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)\top}\right\rangle-2\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}\right\rangle.

Hence, by the triangle inequality, it holds that

|S_{11}|\lesssim\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}}{K}\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)\top}}{K}\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}}{K}\right\rangle\right|.

(147)

By a similar argument,

\displaystyle|S_{12}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)}\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)\top}}{K}\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)}}{K}\right\rangle\right|.

(148)

Recalling the definitions of the matrices
\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(1)}
and
\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(2)}
from Equations (
142
) and (
143
), define the random variables

X^{(\ell)}_{j}=\left|\sum_{k=m+1}^{n}\xi^{(\ell)}_{kj}\right|

for
j\in[n]
and
\ell\in\{1,2\}
.
Let

\varepsilon_{0}=\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\|_{1}.

Since
\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
,
it follows from Lemma
22
that

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(\ell)}}{K}\right\rangle\right|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil}X^{(\ell)}_{(j)},\quad\text{and}\quad\left|\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K},\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(\ell)}/K\right\rangle\right|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil}X^{(\ell)}_{(j)}.

(149)

The terms involving
\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{(\ell)\top}
in
S_{11}
and
S_{12}
can be controlled similarly.
Following the assumptions in Lemma
2
and
3
, we have
\varepsilon_{0}/K=o_{\mathbb{P}}(n)
.
Hence, the conditions in Lemma
16
are satisfied with high probability.
Two applications of Lemma
16
, one with

\nu_{\xi^{(1)}}\lesssim n\beta^{2}\sigma^{2}+\sigma^{2}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{2,\infty}^{2},\quad L_{\xi^{(1)}}\leq(\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty}+\beta)L

and the other with

\nu_{\xi^{(2)}}\lesssim nL^{2}\sigma^{2},\quad L_{\xi^{(2)}}\lesssim L^{2}.

yield that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-40})
that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil}X^{(1)}_{(j)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil\max\left\{\sqrt{\nu_{\xi^{(1)}}\log(K^{2}/\varepsilon_{0})},L_{\xi}\log\left(K^{2}/\varepsilon_{0}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{n}\right)\max\left\{\sigma\left(\beta\sqrt{n}+\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{2,\infty}\right)\sqrt{\log\left(\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}\right)},(\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty}+\beta)L\log\left(\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}\right)\right\}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil}X^{(2)}_{(j)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{n}\right)\max\left\{\sigma L\sqrt{n\log\left(\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}\right)},L^{2}\log\left(\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}\right)\right\}.

Applying the above two displays to Equation (
149
) and applying the result in turn to Equations (
147
) and (
148
),

|S_{11}|+|S_{12}|\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}L\max\left\{\frac{\sigma\log^{1/2}\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{L\log\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}}{n}\right\}\lesssim\sigma\varepsilon_{0}L\frac{\log^{1/2}\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}}{\sqrt{n}},

(150)

where the last inequality follows from the growth rate conditions in Lemma
2
.

By the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|S_{13}\right|\lesssim\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star})\right\rangle\right|.

(151)

To bound the first of these terms, we expand the definition of
\mathcal{P}_{T}
and apply the triangle inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K^{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=2\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\right\rangle\right|.

Let
X^{(3)}_{j}=\sum_{i=m+1}^{n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}^{2}
for
j\in[n]
.
Applying Lemma
22
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil}X_{(j)}^{(3)}\lesssim\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}

(152)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil,

where the last inequality follows from Equation (
140
).

Similarly, for the second term in Equation (
151
),

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star})\right\rangle\right|\leq 2\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star})\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star})\right\rangle\right|

Following the same argument as above, we let
X^{(4)}_{j}=\left|\sum_{i=m+1}^{n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}B^{\star}_{ij}\right|
for
j\in[n]
.
Applying Lemma
22

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star})\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star})\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K},\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}}{K}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star})\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil}X_{(j)}^{(4)}\lesssim\beta\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil\max_{j\in[m]}\left|\sum_{i=m+1}^{n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\right|.

Conditioned on the event in Equation (
140
), we apply the bound

\left|\sum_{i=m+1}^{n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\right|\leq\sqrt{n}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{nr(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)},

and obtain

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star})\right\rangle\right|\lesssim\sigma\beta\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil\sqrt{nr(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)}.

(153)

Applying Equations (
152
) and (
153
) to Equation (
151
),

|S_{13}|\lesssim\sigma\beta\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil\sqrt{nr(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)}+\sigma^{2}\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n).

(154)

Applying Equations (
150
) and (
154
) to Equation (
146
) and using the fact that
K=\Theta(n)
yields

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}\right)\right\rangle\right|

(155)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma L\varepsilon_{0}\frac{\log^{1/2}\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sigma\beta\varepsilon_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)}{n}}+\sigma^{2}\varepsilon_{0}\frac{r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)}{n}.

By Assumption
2
, for all
j\in[n]
,

\left|\sum_{i=m+1}^{n}\sigma^{2}_{ij}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\right|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n}

Using the same argument as above, we can also show that

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{J})\right\rangle\right|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil\sqrt{n}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}\varepsilon_{0}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(156)

We omit the details here, as they are similar to the arguments above.

Since
\sigma\leq L
by Equation (
4
), applying Equations (
156
) and (
155
) to Equation (
141
) yields

\left|S_{1}\right|\lesssim\sigma L\varepsilon_{0}\frac{\log^{1/2}\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sigma\beta\varepsilon_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)}{n}}+\sigma^{2}\varepsilon_{0}\frac{r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)}{n}.

Taking
\varepsilon_{0}=\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\|_{1}
into the above bound yields the desired result.

∎

Lemma 16
.

Consider independent, mean zero random variables
(\xi_{ij})_{i\in[K],j\in[n]}
, where
K>n/2
.
Assume that

\max_{i\in[K],j\in[n]}|\xi_{ij}|\leq L_{\xi},\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{i=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\xi_{ij}^{2}\leq\nu_{\xi}.

Define
X_{j}:=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{K}\xi_{ij}\right|
for
j\in[n]
. For a constant
C_{0}
sufficiently large and
n
sufficiently large, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists\rho\in\left[1,\left\lceil c_{u}n\right\rceil\right]:\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\rho\rceil}X_{(j)}>C_{0}\lceil\rho\rceil\max\left[\sqrt{\nu_{\xi}\log(K/\lceil\rho\rceil)},L_{\xi}\log(K/\lceil\rho\rceil)\right]\right\}=O(n^{-40}),

(157)

where
c_{u}>0
is a sufficiently small constant.

Proof.

For any
u\geq 0
, consider an integer
0<T<n
and any integers
1\leq k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{T}\leq n
.
For any
\boldsymbol{s}\in\{-1,+1\}^{T}
, by Bernstein’s inequality as stated in Lemma
38
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{T}\xi_{ik_{j}}s_{j}>u\right]\leq\exp\left(-c\min\left\{\frac{u^{2}}{T\nu_{\xi}},\frac{u}{L_{\xi}}\right\}\right).

Taking

u=u_{T}=C_{0}T\max\left[\sqrt{\nu_{\xi}\log(K/T)},L_{\xi}\log(K/T)\right],

where
C_{0}=(C/c)^{1/2}
for
C
sufficiently large, it follows that for sufficiently large
n
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{T}\xi_{ik_{j}}s_{j}>u_{T}\right]\leq\left(\frac{T}{K}\right)^{CT}\leq\left(\frac{2T}{n}\right)^{CT}.

(158)

Hence, taking a union bound over all
1\leq k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{T}\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{T}X_{(j)}>u_{T}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcup_{k_{1}<\cdots<k_{T}}\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{T}X_{k_{j}}>u_{T}\right\}\right]\leq\sum_{k_{1}<\cdots<k_{T}}\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{T}X_{k_{j}}>u_{T}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}<\cdots<k_{T}}\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{\boldsymbol{s}\in\{\pm 1\}^{T}}\sum_{j=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\xi_{ik_{j}}s_{j}>u_{T}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{1}<\cdots<k_{T}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{s}\in\{\pm 1\}^{T}}\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\xi_{ik_{j}}s_{j}>u_{T}\right].

Applying Equation (
158
) to the summands,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{T}X_{(j)}>u_{T}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{T}\binom{n}{T}\left(\frac{2T}{n}\right)^{CT}\leq\left(\frac{ne}{T}\right)^{T}e^{(C+1)T}\left(\frac{T}{n}\right)^{CT}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(C+2)T}\left(\frac{T}{n}\right)^{(C-1)T}=\exp\left\{-(C-1)T\log\frac{n}{T}+(C+2)T\right\}.

For
T\leq c_{u}n
where
c_{u}=\exp\{-(C+2)/(C-41)\}
, we have

(C+2)T-(C-1)T\log\frac{n}{T}\leq-40T\log\frac{n}{T},

and it follows that

\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{T}X_{(j)}>u_{T}\right]\leq\left(\frac{T}{n}\right)^{7T}.

Hence, taking a union bound over
\lceil\rho\rceil\in[1,\lceil c_{u}n\rceil]
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\exists\rho\in[1,\lceil c_{u}n\rceil]:\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\rho\rceil}X_{(j)}>C_{0}\lceil\rho\rceil\max\left\{\sqrt{\nu_{\xi}\log(K/\lceil\rho\rceil)},L_{\xi}\log(K/\lceil\rho\rceil)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{T=1}^{\lceil c_{u}n\rceil}\left(\frac{T}{n}\right)^{40T}=\sum_{t=1}^{41}\left(\frac{T}{n}\right)^{40T}+\sum_{T=42}^{\lceil c_{u}n\rceil}\left(\frac{T}{n}\right)^{40T}

\displaystyle=O(n^{-40}),

as we set out to show.
∎

D.5
Proof of Theorem
7

Proof.

Define the events

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\!\leq\!C\sigma\sqrt{nr}\right\}\bigcap\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{C\sigma\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\bigcap\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{2,\infty}\!\leq\!C\sigma r^{1/2}\!\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}\right\},

and

\mathcal{E}_{2}=\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|\!\leq\!C\sigma\sqrt{n}\right\}.

We first condition on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
.
Let
\varepsilon_{0}:=\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}
and recall the definitions of
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
from Equations (
31
) and (
32
), respectively.
Combining Lemmas
2
,
14
and
15
yields

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{0}\beta^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|S_{1}|+|S_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\varepsilon_{0}\left(\beta\sqrt{\frac{r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)}{n}}+L\sqrt{\frac{\log n^{2}/\varepsilon_{0}}{n}}\right)+\sigma\varepsilon_{0}\left(\frac{\sigma\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}}{n}+\frac{L}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

If
\varepsilon_{0}=0
, then the bound in Equation (
34
) holds trivially, so we focus on the event
\{\varepsilon_{0}\geq 0\}
.
Dividing both sides by
\varepsilon_{0}
and rearranging, we obtain

\beta^{2}\lesssim\sigma\left(\beta\sqrt{\frac{r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)}{n}}+L\sqrt{\frac{\log n^{2}/\varepsilon_{0}}{n}}\right)+\frac{\sigma^{2}\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}}{n}.

(159)

When
\tilde{\mu}\leq\log n
, the last term is absorbed by the others.
In the case
\tilde{\mu}>\log n
, to ensure the bound is non-trivial, we require that

c\beta>\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\tilde{\mu}r}{n}}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\sigma^{2}\tilde{\mu}r^{3/2}}{n}\leq c\beta^{2}

for some sufficiently small constant
c
, which is implied by the assumption
\tilde{\mu}r^{3/2}\leq cn\beta^{2}/\sigma^{2}
.
Applying these bounds to Equation (
159
) and solving for
\varepsilon_{0}
yields the claimed exponential bound in Equation (
34
).
Standard low rank perturbation theory implies that the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-6})
provided that
C
is chosen sufficiently large.
Combining this with the high-probability bounds in Lemmas
15
and
14
completes the proof.
∎

D.6
Proof of Corollary
2

Proof.

Define the events

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\!\leq\!C\sigma\sqrt{nr}\right\}\bigcap\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{C\sigma\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n)^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\bigcap\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{2,\infty}\!\leq\!C\sigma r^{1/2}\!\left(\tilde{\mu}\vee\log n\right)^{1/2}\right\},

and

\mathcal{E}_{2}=\left\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|\!\leq\!C\sigma\sqrt{n}\right\}.

We first fix
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
to be any matrix satisfying the conditions of

\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}.

Suppose that
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}
satisfies Equation (
25
) and
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{(0)}
satisfies Equation (
24
) with the same
{I_{\star}}
.
For all
i,j\in[n]
, let the entries of
\boldsymbol{C}
and
\boldsymbol{C}^{(0)}
be

C_{ij}=(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B^{\star}_{ij})^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad C^{(0)}_{ij}=(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B^{(0)}_{ij})^{2}.

(160)

For any
1\leq i,j\leq n
such that
\left|\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B^{\star}_{ij}\right|<\left|\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B^{(0)}_{ij}\right|
, one can instead substitue
B^{(0)}_{ij}
for
B^{\star}_{ij}
and shrink
|\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}|
to
|\widetilde{\Delta}^{(0)}_{ij}|
, so that the new
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{(0)}\in\mathcal{E}
and satisfies

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{(0)}+{\boldsymbol{B}}^{(0)}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}

Repeating the proof of Theorem
7

on
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{(0)}+{\boldsymbol{B}}^{(0)}
instead shows that Theorem
7
still holds for the new
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{(0)}
.

Based on the above observation, without loss of generality, we assume that
\left|\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B^{\star}_{ij}\right|\geq\left|\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B^{(0)}_{ij}\right|
holds for all
1\leq i,j\leq n
.
Under Assumption
5
and by Lemma
23
, we have that the term
C_{ij}
has first-order stochastic dominance over
C^{(0)}_{ij}
.
By Strassen’s theorem
(
Klenke, 2013
, see, e.g., Theorem 17.58 in)
, we can construct a matrix
\boldsymbol{C}^{\prime}
with entries
C^{\prime}_{ij}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}C^{(0)}_{ij}
, such that
C^{\prime}_{ij}\leq C_{ij}
holds almost surely for all
i,j\in[n]
.
Noting that for
(i,j)\in I_{\star}^{c}\times I_{\star}^{c}
,
B^{\star}_{ij}=B^{(0)}_{ij}=0
, we can pick
C^{\prime}_{ij}=C_{ij}
for these entries.

Let
\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}:=\left({\boldsymbol{B}}^{(0)}+\boldsymbol{W}\right)\circ\left({\boldsymbol{B}}^{(0)}+\boldsymbol{W}\right)
.
Let
\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}
be the solution to the SDP in Equation (
72
) with
\boldsymbol{C}
defined in Equation (
160
).
Following the same proof as Lemma
2
, Equation (
27
) still holds for the
\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}
given above, since it only relies on the structure of
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{(0)}
given in Equation (
24
).
We have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{C}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{C}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{C}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle,

where the second inequality follows the fact that the support of
\boldsymbol{C}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{C}
and
\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}
as given in Equation (
23
) are disjoint, the matrix
\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}
is entrywise nonnegative and

\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{C}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{C}\right\rangle=\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{C}-\boldsymbol{C}^{\prime}\right\rangle\geq 0.

Noting that the other results from Lemmas
3
,
14
and
15
holds for any
\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}
satisfying Equation (
27
),
we conclude that all results proved for
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}
satisfying Equation (
24
) remain valid for
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}
satisfying Equation (
25
).
Finally, noting that the event
\mathcal{E}
holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-6})
by Thereom
2
completes the proof.
∎

D.7
Proof of Theorem
8

Proof.

The result follows by an adaptation of the proof techniques from Theorems
5
and
6
, with the key modification being that we apply Lemma
6
in place of Lemma
5
, and Lemma
8
in place of Lemma
7
.
The essential change occurs in the construction of the prior over
{\boldsymbol{B}}
.
Specifically, in the computation of the likelihood ratio as in Equation (
91
),
we replace the uniform distribution on the sphere
\operatorname{Unif}(b\mathbb{S}^{k-1})
with the uniform distribution over the discrete hypercube
\operatorname{Unif}(\beta\{\pm 1\}^{k})
,
reflecting the entrywise lower-bound condition in
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{S}(\beta,n,m)
.
The remainder of the argument proceeds identically, and thus details are omitted.
∎

D.8
Proof of Theorem
9

Proof.

By Theorem
2
, when
\kappa r=O(1)
, the estimation error on the subset
\mathcal{S}
satisfies

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\sigma\log n}{\sqrt{n}}.

Consequently, the Frobenius norm is bounded as

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq n\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{n}\log n.

(161)

By the triangle inequality, the residual matrix
\boldsymbol{W}^{\natural}
defined in Equation (
36
) obeys

\left\|\boldsymbol{W}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{W}^{\natural}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{W}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

and Equation (
161
) thus implies

\left\|\boldsymbol{W}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\sigma\sqrt{n}\log n\lesssim\left\|\boldsymbol{W}^{\natural}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\left\|\boldsymbol{W}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sigma\sqrt{n}\log n.

(162)

To bound
\|\boldsymbol{W}_{\mathcal{S}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
, note that

\sum_{i,j\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}\left(W_{\mathcal{S},i,j}\right)^{4}\leq L^{2}\sum_{i,j\in\mathcal{S}}\sigma^{2}_{ij},\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\boldsymbol{W}_{\mathcal{S}}\circ\boldsymbol{W}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq L^{2}.

Thus, applying Bernstein’s inequality as stated in Lemma
38
, it holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-6})
that

\left|\sum_{i,j\in\mathcal{S}}(W_{\mathcal{S},i,j})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\sum_{i,j\in\mathcal{S}}(W_{\mathcal{S},i,j})^{2}\right|\lesssim\max\left\{L\sqrt{\sum_{i,j\in\mathcal{S}}\sigma^{2}_{ij}\log n},L^{2}\log n\right\}\lesssim\sigma^{2}n\log n,

so that

\left\|\boldsymbol{W}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}n\log n+|\mathcal{S}|^{2}\sigma^{2}.

Taking square roots and applying this to Equation (
162
), we obtain

|\mathcal{S}|\sigma_{\min}-\sigma\sqrt{n}\log n\lesssim\left\|\boldsymbol{W}^{\natural}\right\|_{F}\lesssim\sigma|\mathcal{S}|+\sigma\sqrt{n}\log n.

Since by construction,
|\mathcal{S}|\geq n/2
, it follows that for
n
sufficiently large, the threshold
\tau
defined in Equation (
37
) satisfies

c_{0}\sigma\leq\tau\leq C_{0}\sigma

for some constants
c_{0},C_{0}>0
, as desired.
∎

D.9
Proof of Theorem
10

Proof.

We follow the same strategy used in the analysis of the vanilla SDP estimator.
We begin by considering the case where
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}
satisfies the structured form in Equation (
24
), and proceed in four steps.
In Step 1, analogous to the basic inequality obtained in Lemma
2
, we obtain the basic inequality for the truncated SDP in Equation (
164
),
which yields the key inequality in Equation (
167
) that serves as the foundation of our analysis.
In Step 2, we extend the results in Lemma
14
to the truncated SDP, adapting to the truncated setting our bound for the projection onto the orthogonal complement space
T^{\perp}
.
In Step 3, we similarly extend Lemma
15
to the truncated SDP.
In Step 4, we finally extend Corollary
2
to the truncated SDP setting, thereby concluding the proof under the more general signal model in Equation (
25
).

We condition our argument on the event

\mathcal{E}=\!\left\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\!\leq\!C\sigma\sqrt{rn}\right\}\bigcap\left\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|\!\leq\!C\sigma\sqrt{n}\right\}\bigcap\left\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty}\!\leq\!\frac{C\sigma\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r(\tilde{\mu}\!\vee\!\log n)^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\bigcap\left\{c_{0}\sigma\!\leq\!\tau\!\leq\!C_{0}\sigma\right\}

and we first consider the case where

\beta=o(\sigma).

(163)

Step 1: Basic Inequality and Grothendieck’s Inequality.

By the optimality of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}
, we have

\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\rangle\leq 0.

Subtracting the expectation
\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}
from both sides yields

\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\rangle.

(164)

We now analyze the structure of the expectation
\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}
,
depending on the location of
(i,j)
in the support pattern of
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}
.
First, for
i,j\in I^{c}_{\star}
, we have

\overline{C}_{ij}=\begin{cases}\left(\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}\right)^{2},&\mbox{ if }\left|\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}\right|\leq\tau\\
\tau^{2},&\mbox{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

By Lemma
19
, we have for some constant
C_{1}>0
,

\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\leq C_{1}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2},

which yields that

(Z^{\star}_{ij}-\widehat{Z}_{ij})\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\leq(Z^{\star}_{ij}-\widehat{Z}_{ij})\left(C_{1}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}\right).

(165)

Next, for
i\in{I_{\star}}
and
j\in[n]
, under Equation (
163
) and the assumption that
\widetilde{\mu}r^{3/2}=O(\sqrt{n})
, we have

\left|B^{\star}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\right|\leq\beta+\frac{C\sigma\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}(\tilde{\mu}\wedge\log n)^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}=o(\sigma)\leq\tau,

by Lemma
18
, there exists a constant
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{2}
such that

\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\geq\alpha^{2}(B^{\star}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij})^{2}+\mathbb{E}\overline{W}^{2}_{ij}.

Since
Z^{\star}_{ij}=0
for
i\in{I_{\star}}
, we have

(Z^{\star}_{ij}-\widehat{Z}_{ij})\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}=-\widehat{Z}_{ij}\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\leq-\widehat{Z}_{ij}\left[\alpha^{2}(B^{\star}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij})^{2}+\mathbb{E}\overline{W}^{2}_{ij}\right].

(166)

For notational convenience, we define

\Theta_{ij}=\begin{cases}C_{1}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}^{2},&\mbox{ if }i,j\in I_{\star}^{c}\\
\alpha^{2}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}^{2},&\mbox{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

Expanding the inner product in Equation (
164
) and applying Equations (
165
) and (
166
), we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{\Theta}+\alpha^{2}{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}+2\alpha^{2}{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}\circ\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}\right\rangle.

(167)

Following the same proof as Lemma
2
, one arrives at the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\rangle

(168)

\displaystyle+\frac{5}{2\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},2\alpha^{2}{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Theta}+\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}\circ\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}-\sigma_{\tau}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle.

The first term above is bounded by combining Grothendieck’s inequality (see Theorem 3.5.1 in
Vershynin, 2018
for a detailed reference) followed by Lemma
17
, which yields that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim\tau^{2}n^{3/2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}.

(169)

The second term in Equation (
168
) can be handled by the same argument as in Lemma
2
, with Assumption
7
in place of Assumption
2
.
We omit the details.
Applying these bounds to Equation (
168
), we conclude that when either
\beta=\omega(\sigma n^{-1/4})
or
m=o(n)
, the estimation error satisfies

\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Step 2: Extending Lemma
14
.

To control the contribution from the projection onto the orthogonal complement of the tangent space, we define the term

\overline{S}_{2}=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right),\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}+2\alpha^{2}{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Theta}+\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}\circ\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}-\sigma_{\tau}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle,

(170)

which mirrors the role of
S_{2}
, defined in Equation (
32
) for the non-truncated setting.
Our goal is to bound
|\overline{S}_{2}|
in terms of the estimation error
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}
.

Following the same proof as Lemma
14
, we first establish bounds on

\|\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}\|\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|2\alpha^{2}{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Theta}+\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}\circ\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}-\sigma_{\tau}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right\|.

Observe that since
\overline{C}_{ij}
are bounded by
\tau^{2}
due to truncation,

\left|\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\right|\leq 2\tau^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{j}\mathbb{E}\overline{C}^{2}_{ij}\leq\tau^{4}n,

an application of the matrix Bernstein inequality (see Theorem
26
) yields

\left\|\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|\lesssim\tau^{2}\sqrt{n}\lesssim\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-7})
.
Additionally, we invoke the same bounds as in Lemma
14
, adapted to the truncated setting:

\displaystyle\left\|2\alpha^{2}{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\alpha^{2}\beta\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|\leq 2\alpha^{2}\beta\sigma\sqrt{n}

and

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{3}{2}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\sigma^{2}\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\wedge\log n\right)^{1/2}.

By Assumption
7
, we have

\left\|\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}\circ\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}-\sigma_{\tau}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right\|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n}.

Taking absolute values in Equation (
170
), applying the triangle inequality followed by the above four displays yields

\left|\overline{S}_{2}\right|\lesssim\left[\frac{\sigma^{2}\tilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}\left(\tilde{\mu}\wedge\log n\right)^{1/2}}{n}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}

(171)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-7})
.

Step 3: Extending Lemma
15
.

We now extend the analysis of the tangent space component to the truncated setting.
Similar to Lemmma
15
, we define

\overline{S}_{1}=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right),\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}+2\alpha^{2}{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Theta}+\mathbb{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}\circ\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}-\sigma_{\tau}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle.

(172)

Following the same proof as Lemma
15
, we let

X_{j}=\sum_{i=m+1}^{n}\left(\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\right),\quad\text{for all }j\in[n].

By Lemma
16
,
it holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-40})
that

\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil}X_{(j)}\leq\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{n}\right)\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n\log\left(\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}\right)},

where
\varepsilon_{0}:=\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}
and
X_{(j)}
is the
j
-th order statistic of the random variables
X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{n}
.
The following the same argument as Lemma
15
and recalling the definition of
\epsilon_{0}=\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\|_{1}
, we can bound
\overline{S}_{1}
in Equation (
172
) as

|\overline{S}_{1}|\lesssim\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}\left(\beta\vee\sigma\right)\frac{\sigma\log^{1/2}\left(\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}\right)}{\sqrt{n}}.

(173)

Combining Equations (
173
) and (
171
), we obtain a bound for the truncated SDP equivalent to that given in Theorem
7
for the non-truncated case.

Step 4: Extending Corollary
2
.

Our final step is to show that Corollary
2
also holds for the truncated SDP.
Suppose that
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}
satisfies Equation (
25
) and
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{(0)}
satisfies Equation (
24
).
Following the same proof as Corollary
2
, we assume that without loss of generality,

\left|\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B^{\star}_{ij}\right|\geq\left|\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B_{ij}^{(0)}\right|

holds for all
i,j\in[n]
.
Let

C_{ij}=\left(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B_{ij}^{\star}\right)^{2}\quad\text{ and }\quad C_{ij}^{(0)}=\left(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}+B_{ij}^{(0)}\right)^{2}.

Following the same argument as in the proof of Corollary
2
, there exists a
C^{\prime}_{ij}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}C^{(0)}_{ij}
, such that
C^{\prime}_{ij}\leq C_{ij}
holds almost surely for all
(i,j)\notin I^{c}_{\star}\times I^{c}_{\star}
.
Taking
C^{\prime}_{ij}=C_{ij}
for
i,j\in I^{c}_{\star}
, set

\overline{C}^{\prime}_{ij}=C^{\prime}_{ij}\wedge\tau^{2},\quad\overline{C}_{ij}=C_{ij}\wedge\tau^{2}

for all
i,j\in[n]
.
Following the same argument as in Step 1, one arrives at a bound similar to Equation (
168
), namely

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}-\boldsymbol{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}^{\prime}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{5}{2\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},2\alpha^{2}{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Theta}+\mathbb{E}(\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}\circ\overline{\boldsymbol{W}})-\sigma_{\tau}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle.

Noting that by construction,

\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}^{\prime}-\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\rangle=\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}^{\prime}\right\rangle\geq 0,

it follows that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{E}\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}^{\prime}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{5}{2\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},2\alpha^{2}{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\boldsymbol{\Theta}+\mathbb{E}(\overline{\boldsymbol{W}}\circ\overline{\boldsymbol{W}})-\sigma_{\tau}^{2}\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle.

Thus, applying the previous analysis in Step 1 to 3 with
\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}^{\prime}
yields the desired bound on
\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}
.
Finally, by standard low rank perturbation theory and Theorem
9
, the event
\mathcal{E}
holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-6})
, completing the proof.
∎

D.9.1
Technical Lemmas for Truncated SDP

Here we collect technical results related to Theorem
10
, which concerns the truncated SDP introduced in Section
4.2.2
.

Lemma 17
.

Let
\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}=\mathcal{T}_{\tau^{2}}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Y}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Y}})
, where
\mathcal{T}_{\tau^{2}}(a)=about\wedge\tau^{2}
is the entrywise truncation operator.
Then, the deviation of
\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}
obeys, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i,j}\left(\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\right)x_{i}y_{j}\right|\lesssim\tau^{2}n^{3/2}.

Proof.

We largely follow the proof of Lemma
3
.
Since each entry of
\overline{\boldsymbol{C}}
is truncated at
\tau^{2}
, we immediately have the pointwise bounds

\left|\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\right|\leq\tau^{2}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathbb{E}\left(\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\right)^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}^{2}\leq\tau^{4}.

Moreover, because the entries
\overline{C}_{ij}
are functions of independent random variables, we can apply Bernstein’s inequality to write that for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n}\left(\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\right)x_{i}y_{j}\right|\geq t\right]\leq 2\exp\left(-c\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{n^{2}\tau^{4}},\frac{t}{\tau^{2}}\right\}\right).

Combined with the union bound over all
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
, it is straightforward to show that

\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n}\left(\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\right)x_{i}y_{j}\right|\lesssim\tau^{2}n^{3/2}.

Choosing suitable constants in the bounds above yields the desired probability of failure.
∎

Lemma 18
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
10
, suppose that

\left|B^{\star}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\right|=o(\tau)

(174)

there exists a constant
\alpha>1/2
such that for all
i,j\in[n]
,

\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}\geq\alpha^{2}\left(B^{\star}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\right)^{2}+\mathbb{E}\overline{W}^{2}_{ij}.

Proof.

For notational simplicity, let
\theta=B^{\star}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}
, dropping the dependence on
i,j
.
Recall that

\overline{C}_{ij}=\left(\left|\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}+\theta\right|\wedge\tau\right)^{2}=\begin{cases}(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}+\theta)^{2}&\mbox{ if }-\tau-\theta\leq\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}\leq\tau-\theta\\
\tau^{2}&\mbox{ otherwise }\end{cases}

and

\overline{W}_{ij}=\begin{cases}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}&\mbox{ if }-\tau\leq\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}\leq\tau\\
\tau\operatorname{sgn}(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij})&\mbox{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

Denote the probability density function of
\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}
by
f
and the corresponding cumulative distribution function as
F
.
We aim to lower bound
\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}
.
Under the assumption that
\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}
has a symmetric distribution around zero, this differencecan be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\tau-\theta}^{\tau-\theta}(u+\theta)^{2}f(u)du-\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du

(175)

\displaystyle+\tau^{2}\left[F(-\tau-\theta)-F(-\tau)+F(\tau)-F(\tau-\theta)\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{-\tau-\theta}^{\tau-\theta}(u+\theta)^{2}f(u)du-\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du

\displaystyle+\tau^{2}\left[F(\tau)-F(\tau+\theta)+F(\tau)-F(\tau-\theta)\right]

We analyze the right-hand quantity term by term.
Since by the Assumption in Equation
174
,
\tau>2\theta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-\tau-\theta}^{\tau-\theta}(u+\theta)^{2}f(u)du-\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du

(176)

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau}^{\tau+\theta}u^{2}f(u)du-\int_{\tau-\theta}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du-2\theta\int_{\tau-\theta}^{\tau+\theta}uf(u)du+\theta^{2}\left[F(\tau-\theta)-F(-\tau-\theta)\right].

By Assumption
5
, we have

\int_{\tau}^{\tau+\theta}u^{2}f(u)du-\int_{\tau-\theta}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du\geq\theta\tau^{2}\left[f(\tau+\theta)-f(\tau-\theta)\right],

(177)

\int_{\tau-\theta}^{\tau+\theta}uf(u)du\leq 4\theta\tau f(\tau-\theta)=4\theta\tau f(\tau)+4\theta\tau\left[f(\tau-\theta)-f(\tau)\right],

(178)

and

\displaystyle F(\tau-\theta)-F(-\tau-\theta)

\displaystyle=F(\tau)+\left[F(\tau-\theta)-F(\tau)\right]-\left[1-F(\tau)+F(\tau)-F(\tau+\theta)\right]

(179)

\displaystyle=F(\tau)-F(-\tau)+\left[F(\tau-\theta)-F(\tau)\right]+\left[F(\tau+\theta)-F(\tau)\right]

\displaystyle\geq F(\tau)-F(-\tau)+\theta f(\tau)+\theta\left[f(\tau+\theta)-f(\tau)\right]

Under the Lipschitz continuous condition given in Assumption
6
,

f(\tau+\theta)-f(\tau-\theta)\leq 2\theta\gamma,\quad F(\tau+\theta)-F(\tau-\theta)\leq 2\theta f(\tau)+2\theta^{2}\gamma.

Applying this to Equations (
177
), (
178
) and (
179
) yields, respectively,

\int_{\tau}^{\tau+\theta}u^{2}f(u)du-\int_{\tau-\theta}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du\geq 2\theta^{2}\tau^{2}\gamma,

2\theta\int_{\tau-\theta}^{\tau+\theta}uf(u)du\leq 8\theta^{2}\tau f(\tau)+4\theta^{4}\tau\gamma,

and

\theta^{2}(F(\tau-\theta)-F(-\tau-\theta))\geq\theta^{2}(F(\tau)-F(-\tau))+\theta^{3}f(\tau)+\theta^{4}\gamma.

Applying the above three bounds to Equation (
176
), it follows that

\int_{-\tau-\theta}^{\tau-\theta}(u+\theta)^{2}f(u)du-\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du\geq\theta^{2}\left[F(\tau)-F(-\tau)\right]-3\theta^{2}\tau^{2}\gamma-9\theta^{2}\tau f(\tau)-4\theta^{4}\tau\gamma.

Applying this bound to Equation (
175
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\theta^{2}\left[F(\tau)-F(-\tau)\right]-3\theta^{2}\tau^{2}\gamma-9\theta^{2}\tau f(\tau)-4\theta^{4}\tau\gamma

\displaystyle+\tau^{2}\left[F(\tau)-F(\tau+\theta)+F(\tau)-F(\tau-\theta)\right].

By Assumption
5
,

F(\tau)-F(\tau+\theta)+F(\tau)-F(\tau-\theta)\geq-\theta f(\tau)+\theta f(\tau)\geq 0,

it follows that

\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}\geq\theta^{2}\left[F(\tau)-F(-\tau)\right]-3\theta^{2}\tau^{2}\gamma-9\theta^{2}\tau f(\tau)-4\theta^{4}\tau\gamma.

By Assumption
6
, we have

3\tau^{2}\gamma+4\theta^{2}\tau\gamma+9\tau f(\tau)\leq\frac{1}{2}(F(\tau)-F(-\tau)),

whence

\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}\geq\frac{\theta^{2}}{2}\left[F(\tau)-F(-\tau)\right].

Choosing
\alpha
so that

\alpha^{2}>\frac{1}{4}\geq\frac{1}{2}(F(\tau)-F(-\tau))

and recalling the definition of
\theta
completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 19
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
10
, suppose that
c_{0}\sigma\leq\tau\leq C_{0}\sigma
, then for all
i,j\in[n]
,

\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}\leq C_{1}\left(B^{\star}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}\right)^{2}

for some constant
C_{1}>0
.

Proof.

Denote the probability density function of
\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}
by
f
and its cumulative distribution function is
F
.
If
\tau\leq 2|B^{\star}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}|
, then the bound in Lemma
19
holds trivially, since
\overline{C}_{ij}\leq\tau^{2}
and
\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}\geq 0
.
Letting
\theta=B^{\star}_{ij}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{ij}
for notational convenience,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\tau-\theta}^{\tau-\theta}(u+\theta)^{2}f(u)du

(180)

\displaystyle-\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du+\tau^{2}\left[F(\tau)-F(\tau+\theta)+F(\tau)-F(\tau-\theta)\right].

By Assumptions
5
and
6
, we have

\displaystyle F(\tau)-F(\tau+\theta)+F(\tau)-F(\tau-\theta)

\displaystyle\leq\theta\left[f(\tau-\theta)-f(\tau+\theta)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\theta^{2}\gamma.

Applying this to Equation (
180
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}\leq\int_{-\tau-\theta}^{\tau-\theta}(u+\theta)^{2}f(u)du-\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du+2\theta^{2}\tau^{2}\gamma.

(181)

Suppose that
\theta>0
.
The case where
\theta<0
folows similarly, since the distribution of
\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{ij}
is symmetric around zero.
For
\tau>2\theta>0
, by Assumptions
5
and
6
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-\tau-\theta}^{\tau-\theta}(u+\theta)^{2}f(u)du-\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau}^{\tau+\theta}u^{2}f(u)du-\int_{\tau-\theta}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du-2\theta\int_{\tau-\theta}^{\tau+\theta}uf(u)du+\theta^{2}\left[F(\tau-\theta)-F(-\tau-\theta)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\tau}^{\tau+\theta}u^{2}f(u)du-\int_{\tau-\theta}^{\tau}u^{2}f(u)du+\theta^{2}\leq\theta^{2}(1+4\tau f(\tau)).

Applying this to Equation (
181
),

\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}\leq(1+2\gamma\tau^{2}+4\tau f(\tau))\theta^{2}.

(182)

By Assumption
5
, we have

\frac{1}{2}\tau f(\tau)\leq\int_{\frac{\tau}{2}}^{\tau}f(u)du=F(\tau)-F\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)\leq 1.

(183)

By Assumption
6
and the assumption that
\tau\geq c_{0}\sigma
, we have

\tau^{2}\gamma\leq c^{\prime}\frac{\sigma}{\tau}\leq\frac{c^{\prime}}{c_{0}}

for some sufficiently small constant
c^{\prime}
.
Combining this with Equations (
182
) and (
183
) yields

\mathbb{E}\overline{C}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}\overline{W}_{ij}^{2}\leq(1+2\gamma\tau^{2}+4\tau f(\tau))\theta^{2}\leq C_{1}\theta^{2},

for some constant
C_{1}>0
.
∎

D.10
Proof of Theorem
11

Proof.

The proof follows the same structure as Theorem
10
.
We highlight the key differences, omitting some details.
We remind the reader that
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Y}}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Y}}_{2}
are residual matrices introduced in Equation (
7
).
The matrix
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}
represents the estimation error matrix
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
restricted to rows and columns indexed by
\mathcal{U}
.
The matrices
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{2}
are the noise matrices restricted to the same index set.
Following the discussion at the beginning of Section
4
, the results are obtained under
\mathcal{U}=[n]
without loss of generality.
Under this notation, we have the following decomposition:

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{Y}}_{l}={\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{l}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}},\quad l=1,2.

All of the following analysis will be conditioned on the event

\left\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{nr}\right\}\bigcup\left\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|\leq C\sigma\sqrt{n}\right\}\bigcup\left\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{C\sigma\widetilde{\mu}^{1/2}r(\widetilde{\mu}\vee\log n)^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}.

One can then use a high probability bound obtained in Theorem
2
to show that the above event holds with the desired probability.

We follow the same four steps as in Theorem
10
.
The first step is to extend the results in Lemmas
2
and
3
.
The second step is to extend Lemma
14
to the case where the noise matrices are not independent.
The third step is to extend Lemma
15
to the case where the noise matrices are not independent.
The final step is to extend Corollary
2
to the case where
{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}
follows a more general form given in Equation (
25
).

Step 1: Basic Inequality and Grothendieck’s Inequality.

By the optimality of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}
,

\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\rangle\leq 0.

(184)

Letting
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}=\left({\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{1}\right)\circ\left({\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{2}\right)
, we have

\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}={\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}=\beta^{2}\left[\boldsymbol{J}-\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{J}_{1}&\\
&\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right]

and it follows from Equation (
184
) that

\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}\right\rangle=\beta^{2}\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{J}_{1}&\\
&\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\end{pmatrix}-\boldsymbol{J}\right\rangle.

Thus, following the same argument as in Theorem
2
, we have

\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}\leq\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}\right\rangle.

Expanding
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}
in the right hand side of the above display gives

\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}\leq\frac{5}{2\beta^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{2}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{1}+2{\boldsymbol{B}}^{\star}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\right\rangle.

Among the terms at the right hand side of the above display, we focus on bounding the leading term

\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}\right\rangle.

The other terms can be handled in the same manner as in Lemmas
2
and
3
, and thus details are omitted.
To control the leading term, it boils down to bounding

\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{1}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{2}\right\rangle\leq 2c_{0}\max_{\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\{\pm 1\}^{n}}\left|\sum_{i,j}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{1,ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{2,ij}x_{i}y_{j}\right|,

where we have used Grothendieck’s inequality as stated in Theorem 3.5.1 in
Vershynin, 2018
.
Letting
X_{ij}=\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{1,ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{2,ij}x_{i}y_{j}
for
i,j\in[n]
, we have

|X_{ij}|\leq L^{2},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}X_{ij}^{2}=\sigma_{1,ij}^{2}\sigma_{2,ij}^{2}\leq\sigma^{4}.

Applying Bernstein’s inequality in Lemma
38
, it holds for all
t\leq n^{2}\sigma^{4}/L^{2}
that

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n}X_{ij}\right|\geq t\right]\leq 2\exp\left(-c\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{n^{2}\sigma^{4}},\frac{t}{L^{2}}\right\}\right)=2\exp\left\{-\frac{ct^{2}}{\sigma^{4}n^{2}}\right\}.

Applying the union bound and following the same proof as Lemma
3
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\max_{\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}}\left|\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n}X_{ij}\right|\lesssim\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}.

Combining all the results and following the same argument as in Lemmas
2
and
3
, we have

\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}}{\beta^{2}}+\frac{\sigma r^{1/2}n^{3/2}}{\beta}.

Step 2: Extending Lemma
14
.

Define

\widetilde{S}_{2}=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}\right\rangle.

We follow the same argument as in Lemma
14
, the key difference being that we need to bound the term

\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{1}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{2}\right\|

instead of
\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\|
.
Since

\left|\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{1,ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{2,ij}\right|\leq L^{2},\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{j}\left(\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{1,ij}\widetilde{W}^{(1)}_{2,ij}\right)^{2}\leq\sigma^{4}n,

the matrix Bernstein inequality in Theorem
26
yields

\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{1}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}_{2}\right\|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n}+L^{2}\sqrt{\log n}.

By the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
14
, we have

\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}\right\|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\widetilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}(\widetilde{\mu}\vee\log n)^{1/2}+\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n}+L^{2}\sqrt{\log n}

and

\displaystyle|\widetilde{S}_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathcal{P}_{T^{\perp}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}})\right)\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}\right\|\leq\frac{\left\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}\right\|}{K}\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}\ \left(\frac{\sigma^{2}\widetilde{\mu}^{1/2}r^{3/2}(\widetilde{\mu}\vee\log n)^{1/2}}{n}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{L^{2}\sqrt{\log n}}{n}\right).

Step 3: Extending Lemma
15
.

Let

\widetilde{S}_{1}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{C}}^{(1)}\right)\right\rangle

and define
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}=\boldsymbol{W}_{1}\circ\boldsymbol{W}_{2}
.
We follow the proof of Lemma
15
, with the difference that we need to bound

\widetilde{S}_{10}=\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star},\mathcal{P}_{T}\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}^{\top}\right)\right\rangle

instead of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}
being
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}\circ\widetilde{\boldsymbol{W}}^{(1)}
.
Following the same argument as in Lemma
15
, we let

X_{j}:=\left|\sum_{k=m+1}^{n}\widetilde{\xi}_{kj}\right|

for
j\in[n]
. The key quantity to control is the sum of order statistics

\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil}X_{(j)},

where
\varepsilon_{0}:=\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\star}\right\|_{1}
.
To apply Lemma
16
, we note that the variance and magnitude parameters are given by

\nu_{\widetilde{\xi}}\lesssim n\sigma^{4},\quad\text{and}\quad L_{\widetilde{\xi}}^{2}\lesssim L^{2},

which implies that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-40})
,

\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil\varepsilon_{0}/K\rceil}X_{(j)}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{n}\max\left\{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n\log\left(\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}\right)},L^{2}\log\left(\frac{n^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}}\right)\right\}.
